import json
f = json.loads(open('flows/LangGraph RAG Pipeline.json', encoding='utf-8').read())
nodes = f['data']['nodes']; edges = f['data']['edges']
outgoing = {}
incoming = {}
for e in edges:
    outgoing.setdefault(e['source'], []).append(e['data']['sourceHandle']['name'])
    incoming.setdefault(e['target'], []).append(e['data']['targetHandle']['fieldName'])

print("=== orphaned components (no edges at all) ===")
for n in nodes:
    if n['type'] != 'genericNode': continue
    if n['id'] not in outgoing and n['id'] not in incoming:
        print('  ', n['data']['node']['display_name'], '|', n['data']['type'])

print("\n=== components with no outgoing edge (dead ends) ===")
for n in nodes:
    if n['type'] != 'genericNode': continue
    t = n['data']['type']
    if n['id'] not in outgoing and t != 'ChatOutput':
        print('  ', n['data']['node']['display_name'], '|', t)

print("\n=== key config ===")
for n in nodes:
    if n['type'] != 'genericNode': continue
    t = n['data']['type']; tpl = n['data']['node']['template']; dn = n['data']['node']['display_name']
    if t in ('GroqModel',):
        print(f"  {dn:34} api_key={tpl['api_key']['value']!r} load_from_db={tpl['api_key'].get('load_from_db')} model={tpl['model_name']['value']} temp={tpl['temperature']['value']} stream={tpl['stream']['value']}")
    if t == 'Knowledge':
        print(f"  {dn:34} mode={tpl['mode']['value']} kb={tpl['knowledge_base']['value']!r} in_edges={incoming.get(n['id'])}")
    if 'Embedding' in t or 'Embeddings' in t:
        keys = [k for k in ('api_key','model_name','model','inference_endpoint') if k in tpl]
        print(f"  {dn:34} [{t}] " + " ".join(f"{k}={tpl[k].get('value')!r}" for k in keys))
    if t == 'ChatInput':
        print(f"  {dn:34} value={tpl['input_value']['value']!r}")
    if t == 'Directory':
        print(f"  {dn:34} path={tpl['path']['value']!r} recursive={tpl['recursive']['value']}")

Bash Simulate the pre-flight validator on the edited flow
IN
cd "c:/Users/LOQ/Desktop/Projects/Langflow-learning-project" && .venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF'
import json
f = json.loads(open('flows/LangGraph RAG Pipeline.json', encoding='utf-8').read())
nodes, edges = f['data']['nodes'], f['data']['edges']
connected = {(e['target'], e['data']['targetHandle']['fieldName']) for e in edges}

print("=== what the pre-flight validator flags (required, visible, no value, no edge) ===")
for n in nodes:
    if n['type'] != 'genericNode': continue
    nd = n['data']['node']
    for k, v in nd.get('template', {}).items():
        if not isinstance(v, dict) or k == 'code': continue
        if v.get('required') and v.get('show', True) and v.get('value') in (None, '', []) \
           and not v.get('load_from_db') and (n['id'], k) not in connected:
            print(f"   {nd['display_name']:34} -> {k}")

print("\n=== Google embeddings api_key field ===")
g = next(n for n in nodes if 'Google' in n['data']['type'])
print("  ", json.dumps({k: v for k, v in g['data']['node']['template']['api_key'].items()
                        if k in ('value','required','show','load_from_db','_input_type')}, indent=2))

print("\n=== learned_qa Ingest node inputs ===")
k = next(n for n in nodes if n['data']['type'] == 'Knowledge'
         and n['data']['node']['template']['mode']['value'] == 'Ingest'
         and n['data']['node']['template']['knowledge_base']['value'] == 'learned_qa')
tpl = k['data']['node']['template']['input_df']
print("  input_df:", {kk: tpl.get(kk) for kk in ('value','required','show')},
      "| has edge:", (k['id'], 'input_df') in connected)
EOF

Bash Determine which vertices Langflow treats as run roots
IN
cd "c:/Users/LOQ/Desktop/Projects/Langflow-learning-project" && .venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'EOF'
import json
from lfx.graph.graph.base import Graph
payload = json.loads(open('flows/LangGraph RAG Pipeline.json', encoding='utf-8').read())
g = Graph.from_payload(payload['data'], flow_id=payload['id'], flow_name=payload['name'])

print("vertices:", len(g.vertices), "edges:", len(g.edges))
first = g.sort_vertices()
print("first layer (no start_component_id):", first)

# Which vertices have no predecessors at all -> Langflow treats them as run roots
roots = [v.id for v in g.vertices if not g.get_predecessors(v)]
print("\nroot vertices (no predecessors):")
for r in sorted(roots):
    print("  ", r)
EOF

from langchain_core.embeddings import Embeddings
from langchain_google_genai import GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings

from lfx.custom.custom_component.component import Component
from lfx.io import MessageTextInput, Output, SecretStrInput


class GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddingsComponent(Component):
    display_name = "Google Generative AI Embeddings"
    description = (
        "Connect to Google's generative AI embeddings service using the GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings class, "
        "found in the langchain-google-genai package."
    )
    documentation: str = "https://python.langchain.com/v0.2/docs/integrations/text_embedding/google_generative_ai/"
    icon = "GoogleGenerativeAI"
    name = "Google Generative AI Embeddings"

    inputs = [
        SecretStrInput(name="api_key", display_name="Google Generative AI API Key", required=True),
        MessageTextInput(name="model_name", display_name="Model Name", value="models/text-embedding-004"),
    ]

    outputs = [
        Output(display_name="Embeddings", name="embeddings", method="build_embeddings"),
    ]

    def build_embeddings(self) -> Embeddings:
        if not self.api_key:
            msg = "API Key is required"
            raise ValueError(msg)

        return GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings(
            model=self.model_name,
            google_api_key=self.api_key,
        )
Checked. Here's what you changed and why it won't run.

Your edits
The file went through a Langflow round-trip (every node ID was re-assigned), so a raw diff is noise. Comparing by role, you made exactly two changes:

Swapped the embeddings provider — added a Google Generative AI Embeddings node and moved the three embeddings edges (generate_query_variants, both dedup_merge) onto it. The HuggingFace node is still on the canvas but now has zero edges.
Deleted the auto_distillation.qa_pairs → Knowledge · learned_qa (Ingest).input_df edge.
Everything else — 51 components, all routing, the retry cycles, the ingestion chain — is intact.

Why there's no output
Primary cause: the Google embeddings node has an empty API key.


# lfx/components/google/google_generative_ai_embeddings.py
def build_embeddings(self) -> Embeddings:
    if not self.api_key:
        raise ValueError("API Key is required")
Its api_key is "" and required=True. It sits upstream of generate_query_variants, which is the second node on the chat path — so the run dies there, before anything can reach a Chat Output.

The subtlety that makes this bite: I declared embeddings as an optional input on those components, but optional only means the input may be absent — once an edge is attached, Langflow must build the source vertex, so an unconfigured node on an optional input becomes a hard dependency.

Worth knowing why this wasn't obvious: your Knowledge nodes also use Google embeddings (documents → gemini-embedding-001) and they work fine, because a Knowledge node reads the credential from Langflow's provider settings via the KB's stored metadata. The standalone embeddings component doesn't — it has its own key field.

Two ways out:

Paste your Google API key into the node — best, since it then matches what your KBs were embedded with.
Delete the Google node and its 3 edges — my components fall back to token-overlap similarity when no embeddings model is wired (there's a passing test for that path). Slightly worse variant dedup and merge grouping, zero setup.
Second blocker: Knowledge · learned_qa (Ingest) now has nothing feeding input_df. That field is required and visible in Ingest mode, so the pre-flight validator flags it — the same "missed something" class of error as before. Either delete that node (and its Chat Output), or put the qa_pairs edge back.

Three things you should know
Your Groq API key is sitting in the flow file in plaintext — gsk_rUl8I5c6..., on all three Groq nodes with load_from_db: false. That file lives in a git repo. Rotate that key, then use the global-variable form (value GROQ_API_KEY, load_from_db: true) so the secret stays out of the export. I can make that change if you want.

NAC will do nothing on your current data. Your documents KB was built through Langflow's file-upload ingestion with column_config: [text] only, so retrieved rows carry no chunk_seq — and NAC's neighbour runs key on it. It degrades cleanly (passes chunks straight through, tested), but you won't get neighbour merging until chunks are ingested with source + chunk_seq in the column config, which is what the flow's own ingestion branch produces.

learned_qa has 0 chunks, so that whole retrieval track will come back empty until distillation writes into it — which is the edge you just deleted.

One correction to something I implied earlier: I checked whether the ingestion branch fires on every chat turn, and it doesn't. Langflow's first layer is just Chat Input and it walks forward from there, so Directory and the Ingest nodes are separate roots that only run when you run them. Ingestion and query can stay in one flow.

RagNoContextAnswer → No-Context Answer Chat Output

Both must execute successfully. Because your pipeline has multiple conditional routes, only one output should run for each query.

For the Memory Base itself, messages are ingested only after a flow run completes, and ingestion may happen asynchronously according to its configured ingestion threshold. A failed run does not provide an assistant response for the memory base to capture. Langflow Memory Base documentation

Worked for 13s

I found the likely cause in your exported flow: the generate_answer custom component is silently hiding LLM errors.

It contains:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception:
    answer = ""

So when the generation model fails:

No exception reaches Langflow.
No node turns red.
The component returns Message(text="").
Playground briefly displays “AI.”
Langflow hides the empty AI message.
Only the user message remains in history.

That exactly matches what you described.

Reveal the actual error

Open the code of the generate_answer component and replace:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception:
    answer = ""

with:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception as exc:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Final answer generation failed: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
    ) from exc

Save the component and run the query again. The node should now turn red and show the real underlying error—likely an API key, model connection, endpoint, quota, or provider issue.

Also prevent blank messages

Once the actual provider problem is fixed, use this safer version:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception as exc:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Final answer generation failed: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
    ) from exc

if not answer:
    raise ValueError("The generation model returned an empty answer.")

Your generate_answer component already falls back to the draft when a draft exists:

if not answer and draft:
    answer = draft

But when both the LLM answer and draft are empty, it emits an empty Message. Add the validation after the draft fallback:

if not answer and draft:
    answer = draft

if not answer:
    raise ValueError(
        "No final answer was generated: both the LLM response and draft are empty."
    )

The same pattern may exist in other custom components because your code intentionally catches several exceptions. For now, change this specific block, rerun the flow, and the hidden error should become visible.
Let me verify the other assistant's central claim against the actual code rather than take it at face value.

GENERATE_ANSWER = HEADER + r'''
GENERATE_ANSWER_PROMPT = __GENERATE_ANSWER_PROMPT__

GENERATE_ANSWER_FROM_DRAFT_PROMPT = __GENERATE_ANSWER_FROM_DRAFT_PROMPT__


class RagGenerateAnswer(Component):
    description = (
        "generate_answer.py. Uses the from-draft prompt when a draft exists and the direct "
        "prompt otherwise; if the final synthesis call fails it falls back to the draft "
        "rather than returning nothing. Quality is derived from the judge verdict. Always "
        "emits the answer; the distillation branch is taken only when "
        "ENABLE_AUTO_DISTILLATION is on."
    )
    display_name = "generate_answer"
    icon = "message-square"
    name = "RagGenerateAnswer"

    PASS_VERDICT = "GROUNDED"

    inputs = [
        DataInput(name="state", display_name="State", is_list=True),
        HandleInput(
            name="llm",
            display_name="Generation Model",
            input_types=["LanguageModel"],
            required=True,
            info="llm in llm_setup.py: temperature 0.1, 2048 output tokens.",
        ),
        MultilineInput(name="answer_prompt", display_name="Answer Prompt", value=GENERATE_ANSWER_PROMPT),
        MultilineInput(
            name="answer_from_draft_prompt",
            display_name="Answer From Draft Prompt",
            value=GENERATE_ANSWER_FROM_DRAFT_PROMPT,
        ),
    ]

    outputs = [
        Output(display_name="Answer", name="answer", method="build_answer"),
        Output(display_name="To Distillation", name="to_distill", method="route_distillation"),
    ]

    def _run(self):
        cached = getattr(self, "_answer_state", None)
        if cached is not None:
            return cached
        state = _state_of(self.state)
        if not state.get("answer"):
            user_input = state.get("user_input", "")
            context = _build_context(state.get("compressed_docs") or [])
            draft = state.get("draft")
            if draft:
                prompt = self.answer_from_draft_prompt.format(
                    query=user_input, context=context, draft=draft
                )
            else:
                prompt = self.answer_prompt.format(query=user_input, context=context)
            try:
                answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - mirrors the not-ok branch in generate_answer.py
                answer = ""
            if not answer and draft:
                # Final synthesis failed - fall back to the draft rather than nothing.
                answer = draft
            state["answer"] = answer

            verdict = str(state.get("quality_verdict") or "").upper().strip()
            state["quality"] = "OK" if (not verdict or verdict == self.PASS_VERDICT) else verdict
            state["sources"] = [
                {"source": chunk.get("source", "?")} for chunk in (state.get("compressed_docs") or [])
            ]
        self._answer_state = state
        return state

    def build_answer(self) -> Message:
        state = self._run()
        self.status = "quality={0}\n\n{1}".format(state.get("quality", "OK"), state.get("answer", ""))
        return Message(text=state.get("answer", ""))

    def route_distillation(self) -> Data:
        state = self._run()
        if not _switch(state, "ENABLE_AUTO_DISTILLATION", default=False):
            self.stop("to_distill")
            return Data(data={})
        return Data(data=state)
'''

ValueError Details: SSRF Protection: DNS resolution failed for api-inference.huggingface.co: [Errno 11001] getaddrinfo failed

PS C:\Users\LOQ>

This confirms the root cause: your computer is using the router (gpon.net, fe80::1) as its DNS server, and that DNS server is timing out. Both Hugging Face addresses fail because Windows cannot resolve domain names through the router.

First, test Cloudflare DNS directly:

nslookup router.huggingface.co 1.1.1.1

If that returns IP addresses, replace the router’s DNS with Cloudflare or Google.

Change DNS through Windows
Open Settings → Network & Internet.
Select Wi-Fi or Ethernet, whichever you use.
Open Hardware properties.
Beside DNS server assignment, click Edit.
Select Manual.
Enable IPv4 and enter:
Preferred DNS: 1.1.1.1
Alternate DNS: 8.8.8.8
Optionally enable IPv6 and enter:
Preferred DNS: 2606:4700:4700::1111
Alternate DNS: 2001:4860:4860::8888
Save the settings.

Then run:

ipconfig /flushdns
nslookup router.huggingface.co

The output should now show something other than:

Server: gpon.net
Address: fe80::1
PowerShell alternative

Open PowerShell as Administrator and find your active connection:

Get-NetAdapter | Where-Object Status -eq "Up"

If its name is Wi-Fi, run:

Set-DnsClientServerAddress `
  -InterfaceAlias "Wi-Fi" `
  -ServerAddresses ("1.1.1.1","8.8.8.8")

ipconfig /flushdns

Replace Wi-Fi with Ethernet if appropriate.

If Langflow uses Docker

After Windows DNS starts working, restart Docker Desktop and Langflow. If Langflow still reports DNS failure, add DNS servers in:

Docker Desktop → Settings → Docker Engine

Add the dns property while preserving the existing settings:

{
  "dns": ["1.1.1.1", "8.8.8.8"]
}

Then select Apply & Restart.

Also, your screenshot shows an empty HuggingFace API Key field. Once DNS is fixed, add a Hugging Face access token beginning with hf_; otherwise, the next error will likely be an authentication failure. The immediate problem, however, is definitely your DNS configuration.

Worked for 13s

I found the likely cause in your exported flow: the generate_answer custom component is silently hiding LLM errors.

It contains:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception:
    answer = ""

So when the generation model fails:

No exception reaches Langflow.
No node turns red.
The component returns Message(text="").
Playground briefly displays “AI.”
Langflow hides the empty AI message.
Only the user message remains in history.

That exactly matches what you described.

Reveal the actual error

Open the code of the generate_answer component and replace:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception:
    answer = ""

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception as exc:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Final answer generation failed: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
    ) from exc

Save the component and run the query again. The node should now turn red and show the real underlying error—likely an API key, model connection, endpoint, quota, or provider issue.

Also prevent blank messages

Once the actual provider problem is fixed, use this safer version:

try:
    answer = _llm_text(self.llm, prompt)
except Exception as exc:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"Final answer generation failed: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
    ) from exc

if not answer:
    raise ValueError("The generation model returned an empty answer.")

Your generate_answer component already falls back to the draft when a draft exists:

if not answer and draft:
    answer = draft

But when both the LLM answer and draft are empty, it emits an empty Message. Add the validation after the draft fallback:

if not answer and draft:
    answer = draft

if not answer:
    raise ValueError(
        "No final answer was generated: both the LLM response and draft are empty."
    )

The same pattern may exist in other custom components because your code intentionally catches several exceptions. For now, change this specific block, rerun the flow, and the hidden error should become visible.
